{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01DEm7mtPdfpKs4z53kirzZZ","type":"tool_result","content":"#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Holds every `([validated by: <test name>](path#Lnn))` citation to the test it names.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs           # report, exit 1 on any finding\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs --fix     # repoint each line from the name it carries\n//\n// A line number is the wrong thing to cite: inserting an import above a suite moves every test below\n// it, and the citation silently lands on a different, still-valid declaration. The name is what the\n// author meant, so the name is what the citation records and the line becomes a derived pointer this\n// script repairs. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job both index by it, and a\n// citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than one test.\nimport {readFileSync, writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport process from 'node:process';\nimport {dirname, join, normalize} from 'node:path';\nimport {root} from './lib/repo-root.mjs';\n\nconst CITATION = /\\[validated by(?<label>: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>(?:\\.\\.\\/)+[\\w./-]+?)#L(?<line>\\d+)\\)/g;\n// Any other href shape is refused rather than ignored: the coverage job resolves `./../x` and `/x`\n// to the same file and counts them, while this gate and the drift check would never have read them.\nconst ANY_HREF = /\\[validated by(?:: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>[^)#\\s]+)#L\\d+\\)/g;\n// `xit`, `xdescribe` and `.skip` declare a test that never runs, which validates nothing.\nconst DECLARATION = /^\\s*(?:f?it|f?describe|test)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst SKIPPED = /^\\s*(?:x(?:it|describe|test)|(?:it|describe|test)\\.skip)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst TEST_FILE = /\\.(?:test|spec)\\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/;\n\nconst cache = new Map();\nconst args = process.argv.slice(2);\nconst fix = args.includes('--fix');\nconst named = args.filter(arg => !arg.startsWith('--'));\nconst findings = [];\nlet checked = 0;\nlet repaired = 0;\n\nfor (const spec of named.length > 0 ? named : specs()) {\n  const source = readFileSync(join(root, spec), 'utf8');\n  const specDir = dirname(spec);\n  let changed = false;\n\n# Lore test-command interface (lore_list_tests / lore_run_test).\n# Written for this repo's Jest suite rather than adapted from the Vitest\n# equivalent for a Vitest suite: the report shapes happen to match, but the\n# invocations do not.\n- id: hal-engine-jest\n{\n  \"printWidth\": 120,\n  \"singleQuote\": true,\n  \"bracketSameLine\": true,\n  \"bracketSpacing\": false,\n  \"arrowParens\": \"avoid\",\n  \"trailingComma\": \"es5\"\n}","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dddb36ac-ab14-43e4-a5be-4d1f4b49f13f","uuid":"51b93396-854d-4a46-be09-eef751f1305b","timestamp":"2026-11-02T17:57:21.634Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"#!/usr/bin/env node\n// Holds every `([validated by: <test name>](path#Lnn))` citation to the test it names.\n//\n// Usage:\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs           # report, exit 1 on any finding\n//   node scripts/check-spec-anchor-names.mjs --fix     # repoint each line from the name it carries\n//\n// A line number is the wrong thing to cite: inserting an import above a suite moves every test below\n// it, and the citation silently lands on a different, still-valid declaration. The name is what the\n// author meant, so the name is what the citation records and the line becomes a derived pointer this\n// script repairs. The `#Lnn` stays because the lint rule and the coverage job both index by it, and a\n// citation without one marks the whole file covered rather than one test.\nimport {readFileSync, writeFileSync} from 'node:fs';\nimport {execFileSync} from 'node:child_process';\nimport process from 'node:process';\nimport {dirname, join, normalize} from 'node:path';\nimport {root} from './lib/repo-root.mjs';\n\nconst CITATION = /\\[validated by(?<label>: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>(?:\\.\\.\\/)+[\\w./-]+?)#L(?<line>\\d+)\\)/g;\n// Any other href shape is refused rather than ignored: the coverage job resolves `./../x` and `/x`\n// to the same file and counts them, while this gate and the drift check would never have read them.\nconst ANY_HREF = /\\[validated by(?:: [^\\]]*)?\\]\\((?<path>[^)#\\s]+)#L\\d+\\)/g;\n// `xit`, `xdescribe` and `.skip` declare a test that never runs, which validates nothing.\nconst DECLARATION = /^\\s*(?:f?it|f?describe|test)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst SKIPPED = /^\\s*(?:x(?:it|describe|test)|(?:it|describe|test)\\.skip)\\(\\s*(['\"`])((?:\\\\.|(?!\\1).)*)\\1/;\nconst TEST_FILE = /\\.(?:test|spec)\\.[cm]?[jt]sx?$/;\n\nconst cache = new Map();\nconst args = process.argv.slice(2);\nconst fix = args.includes('--fix');\nconst named = args.filter(arg => !arg.startsWith('--'));\nconst findings = [];\nlet checked = 0;\nlet repaired = 0;\n\nfor (const spec of named.length > 0 ? named : specs()) {\n  const source = readFileSync(join(root, spec), 'utf8');\n  const specDir = dirname(spec);\n  let changed = false;\n\n# Lore test-command interface (lore_list_tests / lore_run_test).\n# Written for this repo's Jest suite rather than adapted from the Vitest\n# equivalent for a Vitest suite: the report shapes happen to match, but the\n# invocations do not.\n- id: hal-engine-jest\n{\n  \"printWidth\": 120,\n  \"singleQuote\": true,\n  \"bracketSameLine\": true,\n  \"bracketSpacing\": false,\n  \"arrowParens\": \"avoid\",\n  \"trailingComma\": \"es5\"\n}","stderr":"","interrupted":false,"isImage":false,"noOutputExpected":false},"tool_result_meta":[{"id":"toolu_01DEm7mtPdfpKs4z53kirzZZ","permission_decision":{"decision":"accept","source":"config","reason_type":"mode"}}]}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","id":"msg_011CgeXvTFmg6PmMGj7x97ni","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_016Lgomu2HrJXYrjHTkFmJFh","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/5e278e084b5b/workspace/target/src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts","content":"import {ToolRegistry} from './registry.js';\nimport type {ToolDefinition} from '../../types/ai.js';\n\nconst weather = (location: Record<string, unknown> = {type: 'string'}): ToolDefinition => ({\n  name: 'get_weather',\n  description: 'Get the current weather for a location.',\n  inputSchema: {\n    type: 'object',\n    properties: {location},\n    required: ['location'],\n  },\n});\n\ndescribe('ToolRegistry.execute validates input against inputSchema', () => {\n  it('rejects input missing a required property before the executor runs', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let executorCalls = 0;\n    registry.register(weather(), async () => {\n      executorCalls += 1;\n      return 'forecast';\n    });\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n\n    expect(response.result).toContain('location');\n    expect(response.result).toMatch(/required/i);\n    expect(executorCalls).toBe(0);\n  });\n\n  it('rejects a wrong-typed field naming its path and expected type, never echoing the value', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    registry.register(weather(), async () => 'forecast');\n\n    const response = await registry.execute('get_weather', {location: 4242424});\n\n    expect(response.result).toContain('location');\n    expect(response.result).toContain('string');\n    expect(response.result).not.toContain('4242424');\n  });\n\n  it('throws at register time for an uncompilable schema, naming the tool', () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    const broken: ToolDefinition = {\n      name: 'broken_tool',\n      description: 'A tool whose schema cannot compile.',\n      inputSchema: {type: 'not-a-json-schema-type'},\n    };\n\n    expect(() => registry.register(broken, async () => 'unused')).toThrow(/broken_tool/);\n  });\n\n  it('validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call', async () => {\n    const registry = new ToolRegistry();\n    let requireLocation = true;\n    registry.register(\n      () => weather(requireLocation ? {type: 'string'} : {type: 'string'}).inputSchema.required\n        ? weather()\n        : {...weather(), inputSchema: {type: 'object', properties: {}}},\n      async () => 'forecast'\n    );\n\n    const strict = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n    expect(strict.result).toContain('location');\n\n    requireLocation = false;\n    const permissive = await registry.execute('get_weather', {});\n    expect(permissive.result).toBe('forecast');\n  });\n});\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4729,"cache_read_input_tokens":52788,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4729},"output_tokens":5,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dddb36ac-ab14-43e4-a5be-4d1f4b49f13f","uuid":"bcbd2459-a527-42e6-9d0c-08ca11b264ef","timestamp":"2026-11-02T17:57:39.745Z","request_id":"req_011CgeXvT2cvpYAHjskCuHH1"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-5-5","id":"msg_011CgeXxL3114Kqoek18ziCY","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_014a9REpvz9b8kGPe6Ysw6kB","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/var/tmp/loop-bench/runs/5e278e084b5b/workspace/target/specs/hal-engine-tool-input-validation/spec.md","content":"# Tool Input Validation\n\n| Field  | Value |\n| ------ | ----- |\n| Issue  | #37   |\n| Status | Draft |\n\n`ToolRegistry.execute` looks a tool up and calls its executor with the model's input. Every tool declares a JSON Schema as its `inputSchema`, and that schema is sent to the providers, but nothing checks the input against it. This spec states the contract the registry enforces before an executor runs.\n\n## Validation in execute\n\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's `inputSchema` before it calls the executor. An input the schema rejects never reaches the executor ([validated by: rejects input missing a required property before the executor runs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L15)).\n- A rejection is returned as a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names the failing property path and the expected type. It is not thrown, so the model can read it and correct the call ([validated by: rejects a wrong-typed field naming its path and expected type, never echoing the value](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L30)).\n- A rejection's `result` and its log lines never contain a property value, only property paths and the constraint that failed.\n- Validation enforces `required`, declared types and `enum` only. It does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults. Extra properties pass through to the executor unmodified.\n- `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name.\n\n## Schema compilation\n\n- A static tool's `inputSchema` is compiled once, when it is registered. A schema that cannot compile makes `register` throw, and the message names the tool, so a broken schema fails at startup rather than mid-conversation ([validated by: throws at register time for an uncompilable schema, naming the tool](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L41)).\n- A function-valued `ToolDefinitionSource` is resolved and validated against the schema current at each call, not a schema cached from an earlier call ([validated by: validates a function-valued definition against the schema current at each call](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L52)).\n\n## Round bound\n\n- A rejection is a tool result, not an exception. The tool-round bound (`DEFAULT_MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS`, 5) is what stops a retry cycle from continuing without end.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":2650,"cache_read_input_tokens":66040,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":2650},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"global","speed":"standard"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"dddb36ac-ab14-43e4-a5be-4d1f4b49f13f","uuid":"ee06288c-357d-47ce-bd3b-d5d3e8d494f9","timestamp":"2026-11-02T17:57:52.890Z","request_id":"req_011CgeXxKo719wA22AJnDxt9"}

This spec states the contract the registry enforces before an executor runs.\n\n## Validation in execute\n\n- `ToolRegistry.execute` validates `input` against the resolved definition's `inputSchema` before it calls the executor. An input the schema rejects never reaches the executor ([validated by: rejects input missing a required property before the executor runs](../../src/orchestration/tools/registry.acceptance.test.ts#L15)).\n- A rejection is returned as a `ToolResponse` whose `result` names the failing property path and the expected type. It does not inject `additionalProperties: false`, coerce types, or apply defaults. Extra properties pass through to the executor unmodified.\n- `execute` still throws `Unknown tool: <name>` for an unregistered name.\n\n## Schema compilation\n\n- A static tool's `inputSchema` is compiled once, when it is registered.
